Poison and inconsistent cleanup fail closed; an unwind sentinel closes the
adapter and privacy flags before a temporarily taken broker is dropped. Focused
tests cover contention, multiple peers, wait completion, expiry, revocation,
missing runs, teardown, poison and unwind; execution remains pending.
Repaint callbacks run before the taken broker is restored, and privacy-loss
shutdown borrows it until shutdown returns, so a panic in either path still
closes the flags before broker destruction. A tick-wide borrowing unwind guard
also closes the flags and drops any broker still in the coordinator if a
pre-take callback panics, without acquiring admission or suppressing the panic.
Command and overflow entry points and the surrounding application input,
logic and UI dispatch have equivalent idempotent unwind cuts, including
post-dispatch repaint callbacks. A latched enabled MCP setting now visibly
requires explicit restart rather than appearing disabled.
It clears startup intents and opt-ins, latches MCP startup closed until an
explicit restart, atomically disarms workflow selections, and defers retirement
of pending startup workers and queued selection closures to normal cleanup.
Workflow selection can independently rearm after cleanup by explicitly
enabling JavaScript after a disabled state is observed; unchanged settings
cannot rearm it, and settings show the recovery instruction.
It does not stop capture work already running or recall an extracted bundle.
Completion alone does not guard the public completion API or other broker cuts,
or establish metadata, final-recipient or physical-delivery safety.

## Serializing outbound queue admission

The app now retains the shared admission guard from its connection, deadline
and target checks through the bounded nonblocking response-queue insertion;
queue failure closes the connection before releasing that guard. A
child-isolated regression pauses at insertion and verifies that a competing
revocation cannot cross the checks. The legacy frame remains non-authorizing,
and broker liveness and final-recipient delivery fences are still missing.

## Closing poison paths before diagnostics

The shared admission-flag helper, privacy gate and MCP stop now apply their
atomic closure and broker-drop cuts before reporting admission poison, without
logging under admission. An isolated panic-subscriber regression covers the
asynchronous privacy callback, both flag directions, multiple peers and
metadata disclosure liveness. At the controller request limit, a poisoned
connection also closes before the same diagnostic. Later cleanup may still
be interrupted; this does not qualify a platform adapter or add a
final-recipient delivery fence.

## Guarding peer wait cancellation

Peer cancellation now retains a bounded, already-consumed cancellation on
admission contention, suppresses the affected wait, and retries its broker
cut before processing further peer events. `Broker::cancel_run` requires the
shared guard; poison follows the existing global privacy-stop path. Regression
execution is tracked with the PR. Cleanup now disconnects under admission but
defers bounded error logging until after releasing it, so a tracing subscriber
cannot reenter the non-reentrant guard from those logs. This does not guard
other run cuts or broker Drop, or qualify enqueue, metadata output or physical
delivery.

## Outbound queue ownership extraction

The existing four-frame IPC response queue and its frame/target guards now live
in the authority crate and feed the same app-owned connection and writer.
That extraction changed ownership only: insertion was not yet guarded through
enqueue, did not bind an enrollment or metadata grant, and proved no revocable
enqueue or physical delivery. Production qualification and metadata routing
remain closed. Focused queue and connection regressions cover capacity,
ordering, closure and guard freshness; execution results are tracked with the
PR.

## Wiring non-authorizing controller enrollment

The local integration work adds an application adapter from an explicitly
opted-in controller catalog to the existing principal-owned broker, and a
native Settings flow that requires a separate choose, exact current-window
local-PTY selection and confirmation. Untrusted identifiers and labels preserve
readable Unicode while bounded and escaped for quoted display; registration
alone opens no prompt or grants access.
Status exposes only connection-owned opaque correlation IDs. Moves, close,
disconnect, privacy loss, newer decisions, conflicting actions and bounded
queue exhaustion invalidate or refuse stale work; queued stale replies close
the connection. JavaScript is not involved.

Production private bootstrap and controller qualification remain
unconditionally closed: synthetic peers and injected qualification are not
platform or client evidence. Metadata association-handle consumption,
separate metadata grant UI/publication fencing, presentation, input, and native
handoff remain unimplemented or unqualified. The controller and presentation
catalogs are separate and `Machine::new()` remains observation-only. The
integration remains subject to combined-tree validation; #506 is untouched.

The assembly's preference-disable regression observes metadata liveness only
after a direct-input commit triggers coordinator revocation, not at an
independent metadata-publication boundary. Future metadata publication must
check the shared stop/privacy/recipient and discovery-grant fences itself.
The existing preference owner still cannot promise a durable off bit after an
arbitrary failed or indeterminate save.

## Guarding enrollment identity mutation

The broker now requires the existing shared opaque admission guard for
registration membership, enrollment identity and proposal-revision changes,
including target and connection retirement and global revocation. Host
lifecycle cuts keep related flags and registry changes together; reconciliation
captures current attachment epochs under that guard. Reconciliation try-locks:
a busy tick retains the broker, defers peer/disclosure processing and schedules
a retry, refusing transient approval rather than replaying it. Observation
approval checks and installs its grant under one try-guard, and startup defers
admission-dependent binding without losing pending state. Completely dormant
shutdown skips the new guard acquisition. Presentation-only updates and
ordinary grant APIs remain separate. Focused contention, lifecycle, startup
and retry tests cover these in-memory boundaries.

When combined with native enrollment, target and controller reconciliation
share the same try-acquired guard; a busy tick does not replay a controller
confirmation. Controller settings report an incomplete view while admission
is busy. Native controller actions now distinguish pre-mutation contention
from other refusals and ask the user to retry; they are never automatically
replayed. A later successful action replaces a stale busy or pending-safety-work
message without claiming to change any existing grants. Test helpers retry
only these proven pre-mutation refusals or an incomplete
snapshot. These integration changes still require combined-tree validation.

This is only a prerequisite: there is no enrollment witness or metadata-grant
binding, no final-recipient/write fence or native consent, and no production
qualification change. Mandatory lifecycle cuts and existing response sends can
still wait for the guard. Nonblocking response delivery needs bounded retained
responses, not a lossy shortcut.

## Sharing the existing automation admission boundary

The existing process-wide admission mutex, opaque guard and attachment epoch
now live in a small dependency-neutral crate shared by the session abstraction
and authority kernel. Session retains its original APIs; both crates name the
same guard. Existing private-field exhaustion coverage moves with its epoch,
and a bounded cross-crate test covers contention and isolated poison. Focused
crate tests and strict Clippy passed; independent source review found no
significant issue.

This is a behavior-preserving boundary extraction, not a metadata grant link,
native consent or delivery fence. Production automation gates are unchanged.

## Freezing direct input through backend reservation

The host-only seam prepares bounded text or keys with the exact tab's core
encoder before modeled review, freezing the bytes, terminal modes and binding
with that handoff. It reserves those bytes on the same tab's backend and
revalidates authority, attachment, modes and publication fences; admission
means queueing only, and post-admission loss is unknown rather than a replay
permit. Synthetic tests exercise the seam, not native behavior.

Independent metadata grants never automatically widen the reviewed readable
closure: cross-flow requires explicit consent and current grant epochs.
Production MCP input, native UI and presentation sources remain unqualified;
writer-epoch installation, recipient fencing and MCP cancellation remain open.
P1 stays observation-only.

## Composing metadata and modeled controller handoff

The inactive host seam freezes exact metadata source guards and the current
control sink before native review, and exposes already authorized metadata
sources in a later control review. Reapproval requires fresh explicit flow
consent and cannot widen the frozen control closure. Slot-specific withdrawal
preserves an independently live other slot; privacy loss and disconnect cut
both. Presentation independently reaps ordinary-grant expiry at every
admission boundary, and active presentation deadlines cannot outlive an
ordinary grant. Unbound metadata replacement cuts control rather than widening
it.

The legacy observation adapter cannot coexist with this seam until queued
frames have per-grant delivery fences. This adds no native metadata UI, MCP
route, real terminal input or platform qualification; P1 remains
observation-only.

## Separating metadata approval from terminal control

The broker can now hold a narrow discovery-only grant independently of its
ordinary grant on the same connection. Separate epochs, expiry and delivery
liveness allow human takeover to cut control without automatically losing
metadata. Both slots still share principal/global ceilings, and reapproving one
cannot refill the other. The kernel refuses combined metadata and control unless
native decisions explicitly cover every source-to-sink flow; stale decisions
cannot survive slot or ownership changes. Independent ordinary/metadata cuts
preserve the other live grant; revision-stale pending proposals release bounded
capacity without refunding their lifetime charges.

Deterministic tests cover routing, closure, takeover, revocation, exact target
replacement and quotas. This is not a metadata UI, MCP tool, enrollment,
terminal write or platform qualification. P1 remains observation-only; native
approval and per-write publication fencing require separate integration and
review.

## Sharing automation preferences across windows

The desktop application now owns the independent, default-off MCP and
JavaScript preference pair across its windows. Its configuration writes and
broadcasts overlay that pair, so a stale sibling save cannot undo a successful
disable. Same-pass conflicting automation actions stop admission and suppress
approval with visible feedback. Preference-save failure or an uncertain result
stops both live features for the process and warns that disk may still contain
an enabled value; explicit retry can save the pair only when the save gate is
healthy and does not resume it. A busy gate suppresses retry, while an
indeterminate save or poisoned gate requires reconciliation and full restart.

This is not a durable failure latch: restart can read an enabled value that
remained on disk, though prior grants are not restored. Unrelated configuration
outcomes, external writers and filesystem namespace races remain outside the
feature-specific fence. Deterministic multi-window and Settings regressions
cover the owner; native usability and full validation remain pending. No
controller UI, protocol operation, listener, worker or production qualification
is added by this preference slice, and the private bootstrap/privacy gates remain
closed.

The local integration assembly adds a regression for a preference disable or
failed save while a separately reviewed metadata grant and a presented
direct-input reservation are live. A successful JavaScript-only disable
preserves the independent direct-control path; MCP disable or either preference
save failure closes it. Another regression combines new/restored secondary
workspace preservation with a stale profile save after a preference disable.
These synthetic regressions are not native qualification.

The save-gate integration tests an unrelated uncertain save followed by a stale
sibling disable or retry, busy and poisoned retry suppression, new and restored
secondary workspaces, and stale asynchronous credential or tunnel-import
completions. Shared preference values are overlaid inside the save gate, and
callers adopt the configuration actually persisted. These in-process checks do
not reconcile disk state after restart or protect against external writers and
filesystem aliases.

## Binding direct input without a workflow

The shared authority kernel can now bind a direct input invocation to an exact
target, immutable encoded-input digest and length while retaining its canonical
parameter digest. It requires the existing native control scope and handoff,
checks the writer lease and exact commit, and permits only one prepared control
request per invocation. Explicit human takeover cuts old grants, runs and
leases; ordinary approval does not transfer another writer. A future native
adapter must explicitly serialize the cut/handoff before a transfer. A still-live
grant may authorize a new intentional repeat within its budgets; a lost response
never authorizes automatic replay, and #524 leaves the durable uncertain-action
interlock to the client.

Deterministic broker tests cover these boundaries without workflow or
observation scope. This does not add an MCP tool, native control UI, terminal
write, presentation or enrollment path; P1 remains observation-only. The real
application adapter and native/platform security and usability evidence remain
open.
## Guarding the full readable-source closure

Re-review found that an unreconciled disconnect of an approved source could
otherwise leave control of a different target usable, and that synchronizing
an already-retired handoff could revoke unrelated principals. The modeled
review now freezes every approved readable source's exact lifecycle,
registration and attachment guards before consent and checks them through
input publication. Retirement cuts affected broker scopes while preserving
unrelated live grants. Deterministic tests cover disconnect, loss/regain,
replacement and guard changes before review, lease and publication, including
serialization with a concurrent transition and first-try review after target
replacement. These tests do not qualify native focus, worker disclosure or
any production input path.

## Freezing the control review before consent

Re-review found that an old native control decision could otherwise be applied
after human input when no handoff was retained, and that creating the first
fresh proposal before cutting a stale handoff invalidated that proposal. The
modeled coordinator now cuts stale handoffs/reviews first, freezes the exact
controller/IME/attachment fence with a bounded pending proposal before native
review, and binds the native decision to that proposal. Tests cover first-grant
and pruned-handoff input, IME/preedit/paste and attachment loss/regain,
cross-channel isolation and first-try fresh approval. Expected-success tests
retry only bounded `Busy` results; explicit contention assertions still call
the nonblocking production path directly. All native and production gates
remain closed.

## Binding presentation input to the original handoff and transport

Independent review found that recapturing a current human-input fence could
otherwise reuse an old control grant, that equal bytes did not identify the
reserved PTY, and that a native barrier error after publication could be
misreported as an unadmitted refusal. The modeled coordinator now retains the
original native-handoff fence with the principal's grant and exact lease/run,
propagates stale handoffs before recapture, and checks the reservation's
controller and backend attachment identity/generation under the same admission
lock. A post-publication barrier failure is unknown and cannot authorize replay.
The UI admission and focus-queue paths refuse a contended lock without waiting.

Deterministic cross-component tests cover typing followed by recapture,
IME loss/regain and pending input, multiple principals, wrong or replaced PTYs,
and callback success followed by barrier failure. These tests use injected
barriers, not native activation evidence. Production presentation, native
handoff UI, direct input and worker disclosure remain unavailable.

## Refusing presentation without a continuous native witness

The controller kernel now separates exact-target enrollment from an explicit
native presentation grant and bounds pending activation, expiry and attempts.
The application reuses its tab command and tracks non-reusable workspace
changes; a modeled admission seam rechecks presentation together with the
existing privacy, attachment, human-input and reserved-byte fences. An
away-and-back transition cannot be repaired by seeing the same tab again.

No current platform supplies the required complete native event history and
ordering barrier through admission, and secondary eframe viewports lack a
uniform raw-handle path. The later-stage MCP capability advertises
unavailability and its presentation call refuses without prompting or focusing.
Injected event tests are not native proof; enrollment usability/qualification,
real activation, response delivery, direct input and Windows qualification
remain open.
## Specifying a dormant controller protocol

The MCP protocol crate now has an explicitly selected catalog for bounded,
typed controller registration and status requests. The default observation
catalog is unchanged. At that checkpoint the application refused controller
operations even with the optional catalog; the later local adapter above
routes them only behind native opt-in and qualification. Requests cannot open
a native prompt or grant authority. At that checkpoint association IDs had no
metadata consumer; the later synthetic-only route is described above. No
production listener is enabled, and production metadata remains unavailable
regardless of enrollment or grant status. Private bootstrap, privacy
qualification and client integration remain open.

## Selecting exact controller targets without granting authority

An external controller's local registration can now be represented as bounded,
connection-owned opaque bytes in the application-owned kernel. A typed native
decision binds one registration to one exact existing target generation and
returns a fresh association ID. Rebinding invalidates old IDs and stale
decisions; even a new selection whose target has just gone stale invalidates
older pending decisions without disturbing the current binding. Disconnect,
privacy revocation, closure, replacement and broker restart discard affected
associations. Identical session labels play no role.

This was a non-authorizing foundation for [#524](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/524),
not a client feature; the later local adapter above remains unqualified for
production.
Separately approved metadata, native-confirmed presentation and end-to-end
admission-time revalidation, native handoff/disarm, uncertain-outcome/no-replay
handling, JavaScript-independent controller operation and platform/backend
capability reporting remain open. The P1 #506 checkpoint remains observation-only.

## Encoding explicit worker input without admitting it

Worker text and key requests now have an inert, bounded adapter to the
terminal's existing paste and keyboard encoders. Key names and modifiers have
an explicit grammar, unsupported chords refuse the whole batch, and captured
terminal modes travel with the exact bytes. This is not a writer lease or an
automation execution path; native authority, target and lifecycle checks remain
required at admission.

## Closing record channels on unknown write outcomes

A physical record write can panic after the operating system accepts bytes,
leaving its actual progress unknown. The output guard now closes and discards
the channel during unwinding instead of permitting the same ciphertext to be
retried. The transport owner must also discard the underlying stream; this
does not enable MCP or replace its disclosure and privacy gates.

## Preparing exact terminal input without admission

The terminal core can encode a bounded keyboard/paste batch against one
captured set of terminal modes without mutating its input queue. It reuses the
ordinary input encoder, refuses unsupported or oversized batches as a whole,
and returns immutable bytes for a later reservation.

This is not control authority. The application must still bind the target,
enforce the automation limit, compare the modes under the live admission
fences and complete the broker/transport commit. No automation execution is
enabled by this component.

## Fencing controller-known human input

The session controller can capture a composite input, IME and backend
attachment fence only while queued input and pending confirmations are clear.
Terminal-bound human input invalidates an armed fence before delivery.
Application-wide IME tracking sees events before filtering without retaining
their text; focus reports do not count as human takeover. Missing native IME
cancellation can conservatively block capture until application restart.

This is not a writer lease, remote-state proof or native approval. An adapter
must still hold the shared admission fence, cut the broker grant and worker,
and validate every target and recipient. No automation execution is enabled.

## Reserving ordered local input without granting authority

The local PTY's existing bounded command queue can now hold an inert,
time-bounded automation input reservation. Its exact bytes are published only
after an application callback commits authority; cancellation, expiry or a
failed callback release no input, and later commands do not overtake it.
If authority commits after the slot is lost, the caller must treat the outcome
conservatively and never replay it automatically.

Component and controlled-PTY tests cover ordering, refusal and stalled
commitment. This is not a control adapter or a lease: native handoff, human
takeover, privacy and broker integration remain open, as did reservations for
SSH, persistent, tunnel and serial backends at this stage.

## Separating shared automation and MCP lifetimes

The application coordinator now prepares the broker and privacy gate separately
from MCP's listener and peer cleanup. MCP-only loss or restart disconnects its
own principals without discarding independent broker state; privacy loss and
application shutdown still close admission and revoke everything. The
unqualified MCP bootstrap remains closed, and the JavaScript preference alone
starts no adapter. Worker integration and native platform evidence remain open.

## Reviewing the captured workflow source

The desktop workflow preview now lets the user browse every captured module
without reopening the selected file. Bounded pages preserve UTF-8 boundaries;
an escaped representation exposes controls and non-ASCII characters rather
than allowing them to change the surrounding UI. Browsing is not
consent, and exact approval, execution and native usability remain open.

## Selecting a workflow without granting access

When JavaScript workflows are enabled, desktop Settings can select a local JSON
bundle and preview its captured digest, requested capabilities, parameter
schema and module names. Capture runs off the UI thread, retains its single
slot until I/O finishes, and discards cancelled or superseded results. This is
not approval: source review within approval, parameter/target selection,
worker execution, iOS selection and native platform qualification remain open.

## Packaging a separate Linux automation worker

The Linux AppImage and Debian package definitions now include the smaller
standalone JavaScript helper, rather than relying on the GUI executable fitting
under the worker's hard address-space ceiling. Package smoke checks its native
Ready frame before providing any source, both from the extracted AppImage and
the installed Debian package. The smoke harness requires Linux procfs and
pidfds for bounded descendant cleanup, and does not execute the helper until
the launcher has a pidfd. A stalled launch, abrupt outer termination, or pidfd
exhaustion during cleanup can still prevent reaping; such failures require
quarantining the test environment rather than assuming cleanup succeeded.

Packaging the helper does not establish trusted executable provenance, approve
a workflow or activate JavaScript. Those application, privacy and native
qualification gates remain open.

## Early application worker entry without authority

The desktop executable now recognizes the private worker argument before any
GUI or application initialization, using the same confined stdio entry and
content-free failure frames as the standalone helper. Extra arguments are
refused. Subprocess tests exercise the real application binary, including
explicit unavailability or the qualified worker's loading deadline and a
supervised, authority-free script.

This does not launch a workflow or establish install provenance. Linux rejects
an application executable whose initial mappings exceed the hard process limit,
so a separate packaged helper may still be necessary. Native confinement,
privacy monitoring, approval and broker integration remain separate gates.

## Capturing a selected workflow before consent

The P2 composition crate can now ingest one explicitly user-selected JSON
snapshot into a frozen bundle. It walks retained directories without following
links or reparse points, bounds the file read, and hands the captured source to
the existing strict graph validator and canonical digest. Changing the file
afterward cannot change the frozen invocation; execution must use the frozen
source without reopening the file. This is not an approval, provenance
guarantee, MCP eval path or executable integration; pending-approval
invalidation, platform evidence and worker authorization remain open. Other
platforms and unsupported path shapes refuse.
The iOS build excludes the unqualified interpreter and refuses worker startup;
compilation does not establish native document access or execution support.

## Freezing workflow targets before consent

The workflow intake component now binds validated parameters to the complete,
one-to-one mapping of opaque names to application-instance and attachment
generations, in addition to the immutable source and manifest. Swapping two
targets therefore changes the invocation binding even if both remain in scope.
Manifest capabilities and requested target scopes are checked again when host
calls are projected. These checks do not grant access: native approval,
single-broker dispatch, worker lifecycle, session creation and child workflows
remain separate, disabled integration work.

Target-valued parameters now reach the worker as exact generation-bound handles
derived from that frozen mapping, without requiring broader session discovery.
The launch rejects bindings made for a different source bundle. These handles
remain references only and do not bypass the broker or native approval.

## Isolated JavaScript worker without authority

The P2 worker snapshots and hashes a bounded static JavaScript module graph
before a fresh process starts. Host calls are typed requests to the parent,
not grants; the application must still bind the snapshot, parameters, targets
and recipients to native approval before integrating the worker.

On Linux ARM64, an owned subprocess passed active denial and resource-limit
probes under its own seccomp policy, including with the container seccomp
profile disabled. The worker refuses unsupported platforms and failed
qualification rather than running unconfined. Installed-helper provenance,
native x86-64 and broader distribution evidence, GUI grants and end-to-end
lifecycle integration remain open; this component does not enable scripting.

The worker's completion boundary also has an explicit admission
acknowledgement: JavaScript cannot settle while an earlier forwarded effect is
still awaiting broker admission. A one-shot atomic claim orders exact prepared
broker commits against watchdog closure, while explicit cancellation serializes
the broker cut before signalling the worker. Promise jobs retain their order
between staged results, and a result that wins an acknowledgement race does not
fail the run. This is a component protocol, not yet proof of application-level
lifecycle integration.

An independently changing target or privacy epoch can invalidate a result after
the broker queues it but before the worker receives it. Host-result frames now
require an application-supplied, nonblocking disclosure guard for every pipe
write. The application must still supply and qualify the guard against its live
broker, recipients and source generations; this change does not enable runs.

The worker can now signal a bounded, coalescing event-wake channel without
calling the GUI from its watchdog. A future application bridge must drain
active runs and handle startup and per-frame backlogs; an unresponsive GUI
cannot block the worker deadline through this channel.

## Framing handshakes before exposing channels

The experimental Noise handshake now has a bounded nonblocking driver for
both roles. It tolerates fragmented I/O and readiness transitions, refuses
malformed or truncated frames, and withholds the unverified responder channel
until its entire reply has actually been written. Raw handshake entry points
are private, so callers cannot bypass that ordering. The physical writer must
truthfully report accepted bytes; a buffered queue is not the write boundary.

This is not peer authentication or an MCP activation path. Independent trusted
human comparison of complete fingerprints, deadlines, cancellation, privacy,
transport identity and platform qualification remain caller-owned prerequisites.

## Framing verified channels without blocking on full messages

The experimental Noise channel now has a bounded incremental record-framing
component. It handles partial writes and fragmented input in both directions,
authenticates complete frames before release, and closes on malformed records,
truncation or failed partial output. It does not supply I/O deadlines, a
disclosure guard or a transport, and it does not enable MCP.

## Exploring channel binding without trusting process names

The observation prototype remains disabled because a same-user pipe and an
executable path cannot prove the identity of a running peer. An independent
Noise NN channel component now derives a full transcript fingerprint at each
endpoint, rejects non-contributory X25519 inputs, and bounds authenticated
records. Adversarial tests cover split handshakes, replay, malformed framing and
failed fingerprint comparison.

This is not peer authentication by itself. Trusted human confirmation,
adapter/client integration, relay and disconnect liveness, native privacy
monitoring and real-platform qualification remain open; the production gate
has not changed.
## 0.15.0: iOS dictation and documents, Dev Tunnel host files and observation

This minor release packages the reviewed iOS and Dev Tunnel work since 0.14.2.
iOS gains on-device terminal dictation with review-before-insert (#565, ADR
0068), drag-to-reorder saved profiles (#554), and authorized shared documents
with local editing, guarded conflict recovery and Markdown preview (#575, #579,
#581, ADR 0069). Dev Tunnel hosts gain opt-in managed daemon recovery (#566,
ADR 0066), a default-off rooted host filesystem with managed grants (#585, ADR
0067), and independently authorized read-only observation for external
controllers (#593, ADR 0070). The relay host now keeps up to four concurrently
open forwarded channels per client so observation streams share the verified
transport, and grant and recovery changes are journaled and fenced so an
interrupted revocation persists. An SSH lifecycle test wait was also hardened
(#583).

## Read-only remote evidence for external controllers

An external Copilot-status controller needs to tail large remote logs without
whole-file copies or terminal scraping. The rooted transfer service alone
could not authorize disclosure to an MCP caller or guarantee safe saved-offset
reads after replacement. ADR 0070 separates default-off observation roots from
browsing/writes, checks native identity and revision before releasing chunks,
and bounds native watches with explicit rescan/ended results.

New daemon generations inject a non-authorizing correlation marker into the
actual shell environment instead of exposing machine-wide process arguments.
Pending changes to both grants are journaled with the upgrade marker, so a bare
retry after interruption cannot restore revoked access.
Recovery is serialized with that installer journal and reloads policy under the
same lock; a concurrent worker cannot overwrite revocation with stale grants.
Managed root changes preserve the tunnel, host key and shell; old shells do not
gain a marker through a helper upgrade. A stacked broker slice adds independent
native per-controller/exact-tab/root approval and completion-time revocation
fences, with JavaScript disabled. External controllers still own parsing,
offsets and privacy. Signed-host, native grant/lock, live controller and
physical-device qualification remain explicit gates, not inferred from fixtures.

## Diagnosing dead tunnel targets and opting into fresh-shell recovery

A healthy Windows relay outlived its exported daemon. iOS presented the daemon
attachment failure as disabled takeover even though takeover was enabled, while
the surviving host lacked evidence explaining the daemon's disappearance.
Lifecycle journals now preserve bounded, private, content-free startup, Windows
breakaway, shell-exit, panic, failure and stop/shutdown evidence. The worker
watches the exact daemon lease independently of relay connectivity and withdraws
stale hosting. Undifferentiated host refusals no longer claim takeover is off.

The owner selected opt-in recovery of unexpected daemon loss, not resurrection
of an ended shell. ADR 0066 records the narrow change: a persisted three-start
budget and exact-generation launch reservation can create a fresh default shell
from the managed worker while keeping the tunnel and host key. Deliberate exit,
operator stop, missing evidence and uncertain creation refuse; automatic client
reconnect remains pinned to the original session. Isolated regressions cover the
policy and publication boundaries; signed service and physical-iOS qualification
remain in DT-05/07. The existing live installation was not changed.

## Shared iOS documents: current Markdown projection and Contents

The next dependent slice connects Edit/Preview to the canonical document
revision. A bounded read-only worker produces a shared immutable Markdown
projection, retaining cancelled jobs until actual completion and rejecting old
results. Views keep their own mode, source selection, scroll and heading
navigation. Compact Contents uses a revision-bound dismissible sheet; wide
Preview reuses the existing Contents rail. There are no Edit/Preview split
panes. The renderer adds no resource loading or path authority.

Tests cover cancellation and close, projection reuse, visible failure/retry,
mode/history preservation, Contents navigation/view changes, external refresh
and no terminal input. The actual-widget gallery includes Preview and Contents
at phone, keyboard-reserved, short-landscape and tablet sizes. These are not
native keyboard/provider/accessibility acceptance. Source syntax/line numbers,
Find/Replace, safe local copy/Save As and shared document dictation remain open.

## Shared iOS documents: local surfaces without terminal ownership

The dependent local-editor slice connects the authorized Files picker and
document registry to a Documents launcher inside the existing shell. Source
views join session chips and the Sessions inventory without becoming terminal
tabs or acquiring workers. Native input, shared undo/redo, dirty markers and
last-view close use the same canonical engine across views. Fresh Files
authority preserves dirty bytes and observes conflicts rather than replacing
content. Failed saves remain dirty; closed native instances are discarded,
not reopened.
Explicit conflict review now offers Keep edits followed by a separate Save or
Reload and discard. Decisions refuse if their captured revision/source/access
changes. Stale refreshes requeue and cannot restore obsolete authority;
terminal-side document-chip closes use the same dirty guard and input gating.

Multiline editing extends the existing form-field responder's caret geometry
and Return mirroring instead of adding another UIKit text stack. Tests replay
local native edits, guard read-only views and modal input, and keep widget
selection independent of shared history. Actual-widget capture is available
at phone/tablet/short-landscape sizes, including conflict review. A bounded
million-character regression checks complete linear geometry and unchanged
cache reuse; short-height actions move into More. This is not provider, physical IME or
accessibility acceptance; Markdown tools and shared dictation follow.

## Shared iOS documents: shared ownership and native I/O first

The approved editor handoff asks for an editor, Markdown projection and
document dictation, but excludes all Edit/Preview split panes. The first
#570 slice extends the existing UI-free `festerm-mobile-docs` seam with a
bounded registry over `festerm-document`, rather than copying the desktop
widget or creating another undo engine. Repeated opens share identity and
history while delayed edits bind the exact view, revision, selection, origin
and access epoch. Dirty external changes conflict; Keep edits does not write.
Save completion marks only the history point actually published, and revoked
access gates further edits even when an admitted save is finishing.

The native boundary now stages bounded I/O through UIDocument without giving
it canonical text or undo ownership. A real Simulator fixture proves actual
safe publication, BOM/CRLF/Unicode preservation, 0600 permissions, stale
overwrite refusal, external refresh and close/cleanup. App-owned Files
mutations and SFTP downloads retain admission through actual worker
publication, excluding a concurrent document save even after a Files view
closes. Real OpenSSH tests preserve existing round trips and prove refusal
does not touch existing files. This is intentionally conservative exclusion,
not per-path scheduling.

This is the ownership/I/O foundation, not a wired Files/editor delivery claim.
The imported proposed artwork and ADR 0069 preserve the native authorization,
keyboard, visual and shared-composer gates for the following draft slices.
The engine now exposes its existing newline normalization for reuse, and the
mobile admission limit accounts for original encoded line endings and BOM.

## iOS on-device terminal dictation spike

The owner asked to dictate without leaving either terminal-user keyboard.
The existing native input boundary now adds public Speech/AVFAudio capture,
while the mobile composition root owns a volatile review/Insert sheet. Local
capability checks and per-request on-device enforcement exclude cloud fallback.
Revisions replace rather than append, native recording has a 45-second limit,
and explicit Insert rejects line breaks/controls and reuses core paste encoding
without modifiers or an implicit Return. Generation and source-tab checks,
native lifecycle cancellation and navigation cleanup prevent late replay.

The owner changed delivery from a physical iPhone to Simulator. An explicitly
labelled manual editor can exercise review there if Apple's local recognizer
is unavailable; it does not fabricate recognition evidence. ADR 0068 and #564
keep device/language/model, offline audio, permission and privacy qualification
separate from portable tests and a Simulator build/launch.

Review found two lifecycle defects. winit maps `applicationWillResignActive` to
`Suspended`, so the first Speech/Microphone permission alert stopped plain SSH
and cancelled dictation; the host now defers that one suspension while a
permission request is in flight and applies it only if the app really enters
the background. Stop also cancelled the recognition task immediately after
ending audio, so review showed the last lagging partial; a short `Finishing`
phase now waits for the final result, an error or a 1.5-second grace timer.

## Rooted Dev Tunnel host filesystem

The bridge's opt-in known-file snapshot reader could not support browsing or
large transfers. The host now has a separately negotiated, default-off rooted
filesystem service: bounded directory pages, metadata, chunked read/write and
mkdir, independent of terminal attachment. Operators choose the root and
read-only/write grant explicitly, including on an existing managed tunnel;
revocation stops the owned hosting workers without replacing the tunnel, key
or daemon shell.

Uploads stage private files and publish without clobbering concurrent creates;
explicit replacement binds to a previously opened file and checks for changes,
preserving a recovery backup when restoration is unsafe. ADR 0067 and the
[wire contract](dev-tunnel-files-protocol.md) define the host/client boundary.
Deterministic tests cover legacy negotiation, streamed files above the old
snapshot bound, conflicts, cancellation and link refusal. Desktop/iOS clients
and signed-host/native transfer qualification remain separate work.

## 0.14.2: iOS field editing and Dev Tunnel resiliency

This patch packages five fixes. iOS profile form fields now support the native
paste menu, long-press caret dragging and the space-bar trackpad (#536, #538).
Dev Tunnel listing no longer drops tunnels whose listing omits port details
(#537). Windows managed tunnel workers retry transient relay failures, and a
worker whose health record is briefly held by a setup rerun now waits rather
than stopping permanently (#539). The desktop Dev Tunnel profile editor is
simplified to Save, Save and Connect and Cancel; saved profiles still connect
when secure storage is unavailable, without keeping the sign-in (#545).

Each change passed its hosted Windows, macOS and Linux checks before merge. The
iOS field navigation was also checked by hand on an iPhone. Live signed-helper
recovery qualification and clean-machine gates remain under #432.

## Dev Tunnel profile editor cleanup and theme alignment

The native Dev Tunnel profile editor was pruned to a single, consistent form.
The heading now carries a standard **Back** control, the stale "profile
created" banner and the "Save & connect signs in…" explainer are gone
(actionable validation, authentication and persistence errors stay), and
**Advanced** sits directly above one action row ordered **Save**, **Save and
Connect**, **Cancel**. The duplicate bottom Save/Cancel row and both transient
"Connect once" affordances were removed from this form (one-off attaches remain
on the New Session surface); the scoped-token **Store token** action stays.

**Save** now routes through the composition root's real persistence: the editor
emits the dedicated `SaveDevTunnelProfile` command, and its handler returns to
the profile list only after the configuration write succeeds, keeping the exact
draft and an actionable failure message when the write fails. It never connects,
signs in, or closes the window. **Save and Connect** preserves the explicit
save/authenticate/connect flow; **Cancel**/**Back** never save or connect.
Form chrome, fonts, spacing and the accent-primary Save button reuse the shared
approved theming, and the narrow layout wraps the header instead of clipping it.

Coverage is automated via deterministic structural render tests for button
presence/order, duplicate removal, Back/Cancel navigation, Save
close-without-connect versus Save and Connect, persistence-failure draft
retention, the legacy Advanced state, and header/control bounds at narrow width,
plus fixture-only themed render artifacts (default and narrow). Native GitHub
device sign-in, live account/host acceptance and cross-platform service gates
are not exercised here and remain tracked under
[#432](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/432) and
[#429](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/429).

Save acknowledgements are bound to their source tab and pending request;
stale results cannot close another or replacement draft. A new profile's name
remains editable after a failed write. Regression tests exercise actual disk
success/failure as well as editor acknowledgements and stale-result rejection.

## Windows tunnel recovery after a relay disconnect

A Windows host reached READY and worked, then its relay transport disconnected.
The bridge recorded a transient error, but the one-shot Windows logon task left
hosting stopped indefinitely. The daemon shell had not crashed: starting the
existing worker restored iOS discovery and attachment with the same tunnel,
host key and shell generation. The old stage-only log cannot identify which
network or relay condition caused that particular disconnect.

Windows managed workers now retry transient failures after 15 seconds, following
the existing Linux policy. Each attempt repeats the original identity checks
and obtains a fresh scoped token; permanent refusals and clean shutdown still
stop. Retries live inside the setup worker so an explicit upgrade can stop it
without the windowless launcher respawning an old helper. Attempt cleanup also
covers pre-ready failures, preventing unattended retries from accumulating
temporary directories or bridge processes. Deterministic regressions cover
recovery, refusal, cancellation and cleanup. The restored live host was not
upgraded or interrupted; signed-helper automatic-recovery qualification remains
separate.

## Windows tunnel hosting without a desktop console

The corrected Windows installer reached READY, but Task Scheduler started the
console-subsystem setup helper in a new desktop window. That window belonged
to the live host worker, so closing it could disconnect the tunnel rather than
merely dismissing setup output.

Windows tasks now start a small GUI-subsystem `festerm-tunnel-worker.exe`.
It invokes only its sibling setup helper's existing `--serve` path with
`CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, waits for it, and preserves its exit status. Noninteractive
CLI and bridge children also suppress console creation. The interactive setup
binary, device sign-in, stdout/stderr pipes, private worker log, exact daemon
generation and user-only service policy remain unchanged.

The Windows bundle includes and signs the launcher; Unix bundles are unchanged.
Explicit upgrades accept only the byte-exact owned legacy task definitions and
the recorded Scheduler snapshot before replacing the action. Existing live
hosts are not changed automatically. Native probes verify the built launcher's
GUI subsystem, console-free child execution, Unicode/space-bearing argument
delivery and exit propagation without creating cloud resources or tasks.
Full live-service upgrade and containing-job survival remain separate gates.

## 0.14.1: Windows Dev Tunnel startup correction

This patch packages #515's Windows host-shell correction. Managed and foreground
setup now share the daemon's existing PowerShell-preferring discovery instead
of supplying a CMD override; the default shell retains its arguments while
accepting the user's home working directory. Unix login-shell behavior and
explicit-shell argument restrictions are unchanged.

The change passed its hosted Windows, macOS and Linux checks before merge,
and its isolated native Windows regression verified interactive output and the
requested directory. This does not migrate an already-dead installation
generation or qualify shell survival after a containing Windows job closes.
Those lifecycle and clean-machine gates remain under #432.

## Windows Dev Tunnel shell exited before its worker started

After task registration was repaired, a Windows install reached
`service.worker_not_running`. The task had launched correctly: its private log
showed a permanent refusal because the exact daemon generation was already
gone. The daemon had cleaned up its registry and lease, pointing to an orderly
exit rather than proving the nearby job-breakaway warning was responsible.

Isolated launches with the published helper reproduced the actual difference:
the managed installer built `System32/cmd.exe`, and CMD immediately exited.
The native `System32\cmd.exe` spelling stayed interactive under the same job
restriction. Rather than retaining a separate CMD default, both Windows setup
paths now leave shell selection to the daemon's existing local-shell discovery:
prefer `pwsh.exe` from the user's Store alias, PATH or standard installation
directories, then fall back to COMSPEC or Windows PowerShell. No PowerShell
installation is required and shell profiles remain enabled.

The daemon now accepts a working directory with its default shell, preserving
home-directory startup and the discovered shell's arguments; shell-specific
arguments still require an explicit shell. Deterministic regressions cover
both launch subcommands and existing PowerShell discovery/fallback policy.
An opt-in native regression starts the default shell in an isolated registry
and verifies actual interactive output and the requested working directory.
No failed installation is silently replaced or retargeted, and survival after
the containing job closes remains a separate Windows lifetime limitation.

## Shared automation authority before external access

The owner authorized the P1-P3 MCP/JavaScript campaign in
[#501](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/501), with an observation-only MCP PR
checkpoint before isolated JavaScript and terminal control. The first foundation
is a memory-only application-owned authority kernel plus independent default-off
configuration fields. Channels, targets, grants, nested runs, budgets and input
lease epochs have typed state and explicit refusal/completion boundaries.
Cancellation never claims to roll back admitted shell input.

Deterministic tests cover hostile delegation, replacement/revocation races,
disclosure, shared quotas, unsettled work and idempotent starts. They do not
qualify transport authentication, worker confinement or native UI/controller
integration. No server/interpreter is enabled. iOS and unsupported desktop
execution remain unavailable until their separate platform gates can be proved;
P4 is not pulled into this campaign.

The observation prototype excludes text-mode SFTP: its worker can continue
producing local output after the SSH transport or SFTP subsystem is lost, and
its backend cannot yet invalidate automation authority at that boundary. Other
session types retain their own attachment-generation checks. All MCP bootstrap
paths remain fail-closed pending authenticated native pairing.

The Windows privacy monitor's listener previously released its permit before
TLS teardown and could detach a worker or silently leak a notification buffer
after uncertain cancellation. A separate ownership prerequisite now uses one
process-lifetime reaper and a single listener slot, keeps native outcomes and
the notification buffer owned through actual join, and exposes pending/unknown
cleanup without blocking monitor close. Its fault-injection fixtures target
stalled teardown, failed-submission storage and once-latched supervisor
callback attempts. The portable fixtures passed on macOS; Windows code was
cross-checked but not run natively. A callback can
block on the admission lock while an admitted operation continues. Neither
cleanup status nor callback delivery qualifies admission, privacy, or
final-recipient fencing; the coordinator remains unqualified and bootstrap
remains closed.

The application now retains the Windows monitor's cleanup result through
listener join and slot release, including cancelled or failed startup, and
blocks restart on unresolved cleanup. Native privacy-loss dispatch marks an
early gate that private input and transport writes recheck even while the
admission lock is busy. Lock and client-exit cuts are explicitly event-driven: notification
delay has no documented upper bound, and previously admitted effects cannot be
undone.
These changes do not qualify native notification coverage, caller identity or
the final recipient, and they do not enable MCP.

## Verify the Dev Tunnel installer before execution

The setup panel could display the exact embedded installer for review, but its
copied command downloaded a release script straight into an interpreter.
Checks inside that script protected later helper downloads, not the installer
itself. The shared desktop/iOS command generator now pins a SHA-256 from its
embedded script and refuses different bytes before execution. Unix downloads
use a temporary file with cleanup; PowerShell hashes and interprets the same
in-memory bytes. No remotely supplied checksum can change the expected hash.

Local shell regressions cover matching, modified and empty downloads, download
failure and preservation of quoted options without provisioning a tunnel.
Clean-machine release/service qualification remains separate.

## Windows Dev Tunnel task XML encoding

A native Windows host install reached authenticated provisioning and session
startup, then stopped at `service.task_create`. The generated UTF-8 XML failed
Scheduler import with `0x8004131A` ("unable to switch the encoding"). Removing
the declaration imported but corrupted non-ASCII text, so task files now use
UTF-16LE with a BOM and matching declaration. Explicit upgrades recognize both
the old exact owned definition and the corrected encoding without relaxing
task ownership or overwriting foreign definitions.

Deterministic coverage preserves accented and supplementary Unicode through
the task-file boundary. The opt-in native prerequisite smoke validates the
generated file through Scheduler without registering a task, and is included
in the optional Windows runner. A controlled disabled-task import reproduced
the old failure and verified Unicode preservation with UTF-16LE; no task was
run and all probe tasks were removed. This is task-registration evidence, not
clean-machine/service-lifecycle acceptance.

The 2026-10-30 isolated macOS-to-Debian-ARM64 Pi Dev Tunnel test now passes
actual GUI shell input/output, including the integrated native service-app flow.
Relay WebSockets explicitly
select a local Rustls provider to survive the desktop's combined ring/aws-lc
feature graph without changing process defaults or certificate checks. Native
GitHub account validation previously succeeded while management lookup returned
HTTP 401 for fesTerm's own OAuth registration (#429); that historical failure
does not describe the integrated SDK-published service application.
Reusable per-user host setup, public profile
import and six-platform helper bundles/installers are implemented locally, not
published; native signing and clean-machine qualification remain release
prerequisites under [#432](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/432).
Hosted Windows diagnosis distinguished servicing-owned system roots from
foreign-account grants in runner-profile temporary storage. Private-ancestor
policy now admits only the exact servicing SID in addition to the prior
principals, rejects foreign directory-self deletion, and preserves both
current-user-only final objects and the separate document-save staging policy.
Policy-validated user/system-temporary fixtures avoid changing runner ACLs or
requiring elevation on an ordinary user installation. Native Windows
installer/service acceptance remains pending under #432.
The first corrected Windows run passed all 26 bridge-library tests, then exposed
a remaining binary fixture rooted in the checkout. All positive private-storage
fixtures now share the test-only builder across library/setup/bridge roots.
Managed fixtures retain native runtime spelling and ordinary paths, and
health/worker locking reuses the daemon's native contention classifier.
Publication and broader qualification remain gated on current
Visual Studio-family usage-rights clarification; the owner authorized bounded
service-application research and integration tests. The same-valid-token
comparison for the former registration returned HTTP 401 with
or without fesTerm's public client ID; that discrepancy is not a proven fix.
An owner-authorized isolated follow-up using the SDK-published Dev Tunnels
GitHub App now passes GitHub account verification and account/existing-tunnel
metadata HTTP 200 with both ordinary and client-ID-qualified authorization
([evidence](https://github.com/fes/fesTerm/issues/429#issuecomment-6527525291)).
That diagnostic established an accepted credential path without changing product
registration or saved credentials; connect tokens and refresh/persistence were
not exercised. The owner's subsequent full-integration request now selects the
SDK-published service application in the experimental product, binds private
vault identity V2 to application and numeric account, and requires explicit
sign-in for legacy/mismatched bindings. The standard code button now copies and
opens GitHub in one click, with no display-time clipboard/browser action.
The owner subsequently confirmed native sign-in, one-click clipboard/browser
delivery, approved pinned attachment and real Pi shell input/output. A private
leased native-store checker passed real secretless refresh, renewed-pair
save/reload, account reverification and fresh scoped-token discovery with the
saved host binding unchanged. The owner then confirmed saved-profile reconnect
after desktop restart without another GitHub sign-in. Actual expiry/revocation,
two-account, other-platform and keyboard/accessibility qualification remain
separate from this partial DT-07 result and the unresolved usage-rights/release
gates.

**Status:** Active project story; detailed acceptance evidence remains in
[`milestone-acceptance-record.md`](milestone-acceptance-record.md).

## Mobile transport lifetime must not depend on the visible workspace

The development-device packager now also accepts an explicit `--live-ssh`
selection, allowing the integrated transient SSH prototype to reach an owned
physical device using existing local development-signing prerequisites. The
offline default, minimal entitlements and private provisioning output remain
unchanged. Portable tests cover feature-qualified preflight/build selection,
local-network metadata and mode-labelled pending/signed receipts; they do not
establish actual signing, installation or the MOB-01 through MOB-06 native
device/live-SSH acceptance still required by #426.

Independent review of the opt-in live-SSH prototype found two composition gaps:
Files/README stopped draining transport events, and suspension cleanup waited
for a redraw that the native host intentionally suppresses. Output and core
replies now advance independently of the selected workspace, once per UI
frame even when egui retries layout. A main-thread suspension handle retires
transport and clears sensitive prompts directly from the native callback,
without waiting for a worker or touching terminal ownership. Fixture reset
preserves that callback binding.

Headless/fake-session regressions prove hidden-workspace replies, the frame
budget and cancellation without another UI frame. Independent architectural
review approved the feature-gated prototype basis, not mobile product support:
actual SSH/device, keyboard/touch and physical suspension evidence remain
MOB-06 obligations.

## Remote access without making the daemon a network service

The owner approved an explicit installer-owned user auto-start service, stable
host identity and idempotent reruns. Healthy reruns reuse the original private
tunnel and exact shell generation; stale or unexplained partial state refuses
instead of creating replacements. Service stop removes its bridge process
group, not the independently started daemon. The public JSON paste flow previews
the server/fingerprint and requires a new-profile confirmation, with credentials
and host approval remaining separate. A real isolated macOS dummy-worker service
smoke passed registration, repeat/start/collision checks and child shutdown;
this is not real-cloud, signed-release or Linux/Windows service qualification.

The GitHub device prompt now renders its short user code as a monospace
button. Explicit activation copies that code through the ordinary clipboard
output path without approving the account or changing the waiting state.
Normal and narrow headless dialogs cover no idle/press-only writes, exact
release activation and retained sign-in state. Native macOS clipboard delivery and browser opening were owner-confirmed;
keyboard/screen-reader usability remains DT-07 evidence.

The existing VS Code tunnel proposal depended on Microsoft's distributed
server, leaving separate licensing, identity and internal-protocol gates.
The owner requested an independent Dev Tunnels route instead. Proposed
[ADR 0053](adr/0053-separate-dev-tunnels-session-bridge.md) puts cloud transport
and authentication in a separate same-user bridge while `festerm-sessiond`
retains protected local IPC, its shell and authoritative recovery state.
Bridge restarts can therefore be separated from shell lifetime; daemon/ConPTY
hot upgrades are not implied.

The selected experimental bootstrap uses explicitly provisioned scoped tokens
from an official GitHub-authenticated Dev Tunnels CLI first, not borrowed
VS Code credentials or an unqualified native Entra registration.
Implementation, bounded SDK integration, service-use suitability and real
remote/native evidence remain prerequisites. This proposal changes no runtime
behavior or milestone acceptance status.

The subsequent implementation initially required explicit Cargo features.
The owner then requested ordinary desktop availability and an isolated
interactive test instance. Desktop and bridge builds now include those
features by default, while connection/host startup still requires explicit
intent and credentials. This changes neither the experimental status nor the
outstanding real-relay, two-account and native qualification obligations.

## Local document links without an OS-opening capability

Copilot could emit real OSC 8 file links while fesTerm silently discarded
their metadata under its web-only policy. The repair preserves narrowly
validated absolute local-file metadata without widening the web URL opener.
Modifier/context activation pins its source session generation and opens only
an internal document from a local session. Decoded destination previews and
Copy path make the actual file visible rather than trusting its label.

The file-open path walks retained no-follow directories and a regular-file
handle, refusing aliases, device/network spellings and Windows mapped network
roots before document reads. It shares existing bounded text validation and
retains unsaved buffers when a document is already open. Remote OSC 8 mapping
and native usability qualification remain explicit follow-ups, not implicit
permissions granted by a localhost URI.

PR review caught that automatic freshness/reload and save-conflict reads could
lose the initial no-follow policy. Retained source authority now preserves it
through those paths, ordinary saves and buffer reuse. Replacement-parent
identity is checked before opening a child, while legitimate regular-file
replacement and dirty-buffer conflict handling remain supported.

## v0.14.0: iOS terminal keyboards and self-healing Linux tunnel hosts

iOS gains a header bar of session chips that scrolls sideways. Long-press a chip
to close its session after a confirmation, or swipe a row left in Sessions.
A switchable terminal keyboard (Hold symbols or Shell row) can replace the
built-in keyboard. The choice persists, the globe key switches back, and the
Extended keys panel adds function and navigation keys. Every app-owned key
shows a press callout and repeats when held, and one bluer palette covers the
keyboards, accessory row and header. Shift, local clipboard shortcuts and Force
redraw arrive on iOS, and Dev Tunnel tabs keep their attachment and re-verify
the host when the app resumes. A latched Shift or Ctrl no longer swallows
Return, Backspace or Esc.

Linux Dev Tunnel hosts now restart their worker after transient failures, but
not after refusals that need the operator. Every host keeps a small private
event log of starts, connections and exits. Rerunning setup migrates an older
no-restart unit in place without stopping a running worker. The Windows host
task XML now uses the correct encoding, and installer scripts are verified
before they run.

## v0.13.3: Friendly Dev Tunnel names

Host setup accepts an optional friendly name for a new Dev Tunnel. Desktop
and iOS show and edit that name in their tunnel pickers, and selecting a
tunnel prefills an editable profile name while keeping the tunnel ID for
connections. On iOS, the prefilled name respects the 80-character profile
name limit.

## v0.13.2: Dev Tunnel auto-reconnect, takeover and host upgrade

Dev Tunnel tabs on desktop and iOS reconnect automatically when the app or
tab becomes active again (per-profile "Reconnect automatically", on by
default); an automatic reconnect only reclaims its own stale attachment. The
bridge now serves relay clients concurrently so a second device can take
over, tunnels can be deleted from the desktop picker and iOS editor, iOS
gains Small/Normal/Large terminal text and relay TLS fixes, and existing
hosts can move to the new helpers with `install-dev-tunnel --upgrade`
(`-Upgrade` on Windows) while keeping the tunnel, host key and live shell.

## v0.13.1: Dev Tunnel picker fills the form and iOS 27 launch fix

Picking a tunnel from "Pick from my tunnels" now fills the Tunnel name or id
of the profile editor or New Session form, and New Session gains the button.
The iOS app adopts the UIScene lifecycle so it launches on iOS 27, and the
native daemon smoke tests send the FSA3 admission again.

## v0.13.0: in-app host setup and ephemeral Dev Tunnel connect

This release adds the reviewable "Set up a Dev Tunnel host" panel (embedded
installer scripts pinned to the app version, copy command, copy or view
script), a compact ephemeral Dev Tunnel connect form in New Session
(takeover default on, optional save as profile), a profile editor aligned
with it, and clearer device-code and View script buttons. The iOS app gains
the matching host-setup sheet, SSH/SFTP/Dev Tunnel flows and app lock;
iOS is not distributed through this release.

## v0.12.0: Dev Tunnel one-line setup, deliberate takeover, and the iOS app

This release packages the per-user Dev Tunnel host one-line installers and
six-platform bridge bundles, deliberate local/remote session takeover (default
on, `--no-takeover` / `-NoTakeover` to disable) with backward-compatible
`FSA3`/`FSA4` admission so existing sessions are not orphaned, and the iOS
app: SSH, SFTP, Dev Tunnels, Markdown viewing, app lock, and profile/Keychain
storage. Dev Tunnels remain subject to the accepted, revocable licensing risk
recorded in ADR 0053. iOS real-device, TestFlight, live Microsoft Dev Tunnels
service, and Face ID qualification remain open; serial is not part of iOS.

## v0.11.1: recovering existing native sessions safely

This patch release packages the native-session upgrade repair and confirmed
termination action merged after v0.11.0. Recovery explicitly supports the
released schema-2 snapshot layout without restarting existing shells, while
new helpers continue writing schema 3. Native Running Sessions gains deliberate
termination with a Cancel-default confirmation and exact-generation checks
that protect replacements and newly attached sessions.

Release preparation synchronizes workspace, lockfile, packaging and versioned
Windows-helper metadata, and makes headless CI select its intended software
Vulkan driver explicitly. Application behavior is unchanged by those
preparations; signed-package and native usability qualification remain open
under CP-11/CP-12, and other deferred acceptance claims are not broadened.

## Selecting the intended software Vulkan driver in headless CI

The Ubuntu 26 application suite intermittently crashed even with optional
Vulkan object naming disabled. An exact-source GDB run reproduced SIGSEGV in
the loader's `vkGetDeviceProcAddr` during device creation while another test
was enumerating physical devices and closing a driver library. Two complete
passes before the third crash showed why a green retry alone was insufficient.

Headless quality and release validation now select the installed Lavapipe ICD
explicitly instead of enumerating unrelated host hardware drivers. Missing or
ambiguous software-driver manifests fail before tests start. The actual
software Vulkan pixel tests, wgpu validation and parallel test execution remain
enabled, without production renderer changes, automatic test retries or weaker
assertions. This confines the test environment to its intended renderer; it
does not claim to repair the system Vulkan loader.

## Native-session upgrade compatibility and deliberate termination

The v0.11.0 selection-generation field changed the positional recovery snapshot
from schema 2 to schema 3, inadvertently blocking existing v0.10.4 daemons.
The reader now explicitly decodes the frozen schema-2 layout, initializes the
new frontend generation, and applies the same structural/allocation limits
before adopting it. Existing helpers keep writing schema 2; no shell restart
or registry relabelling is needed. Independent fixtures produced by the
unmodified released core cover primary/alternate screens, unfinished input,
continued output, resize/history, and invalid state. New helpers still write
only schema 3; unknown layouts are refused rather than guessed.

The native Running Sessions rows now have an overflow/right-click Terminate
action. Confirmation names the destructive target and defaults to Cancel.
An off-frame bounded helper request checks the selected generation and its
unattached state under the registry lock before reusing existing process-tree
termination and cleanup. This prevents a stale menu from killing a replacement
or newly attached session. Errors remain visible and discovery refreshes;
saved definitions and tmux/Screen behavior are unchanged. Native signed-package
and usability qualification remain open under CP-11/CP-12.

## v0.11.0: deliberate copying, portable Markdown and bounded native rendering

This feature release packages the merged capabilities and repairs since
v0.10.4. Terminal selection is layout-aware and explicit about copy intent,
including ordered cross-row clipboard output and document-owned Copy menus.
Saved-profile replacement preserves the selected profile identity, transient
SSH credentials are cleared across all relevant paths, and optional terminal
fonts are delivered separately while saved choices reconcile safely on
upgrade.

Markdown Preview and Split views gain restricted rich clipboard output plus
exact source-copy actions, document-local scroll restoration, bounded native
Mermaid previews, single-submission table dividers and final code-line
galley bounds. Copy payload ownership, parser revisions and local-resource
authority remain explicit; literal HTML is escaped and copying grants no
network or filesystem access.

The accepted ADR 0051 implementation also retains an exact static leading
paint-job prefix during native WARP rendering and submits only the changing
suffix. Source-matched Windows qualification measured lower process CPU in
the covered Markdown and Edit cases while preserving the existing pixel,
resource, admission and fallback guards. Broader hardware, physical latency
and freeze-repair claims remain outside that evidence.

Release preparation changes only synchronized workspace, lockfile, packaging
and versioned Windows-helper metadata plus this narrative. It does not change
source behavior or broaden any deferred native, accessibility, DPI, mobile or
CoreSimulator qualification claim.

## Removing wall-clock races from CI evidence

A survey of 500 recent workflow runs separated ordinary failing PR iterations
from repeated same-source failures. The repaired cases were oracle races rather
than demonstrated product failures. Windows issue #412 recorded one
independently constructed document highlighted while its comparison document
used plain text. Their separate 40 ms production parse budgets provide a
concrete race path, but the hosted receipt did not instrument the parser deeply
enough to prove that causal timing. The comparison was invalid regardless:
production views of one document share syntax state. The oracle now shares
that document. A feature-gated test-support parse primes deterministic spans
without the production deadline, and the live oracle still requires populated
syntax spans before retaining complete shape, accessibility, command and
cache-invalidation comparisons. Production parsing keeps its 40 ms bound.
Sibling input/payload oracles use explicitly plain-text fixtures because their
independent documents and duplicate event streams are the behavior under test;
`ordered_layout_sweep_preserves_utf8_syntax_and_find_formats` retains
deterministic syntax/find formatting coverage.

The Save As clipping regression and sibling Save As/Open picker layout checks
similarly polled real background directory workers for only about 400 ms.
Their owned fixtures now drive the existing paused-loader seam synchronously
and still perform real directory enumeration before the full clipping/layout
assertions. Neither repair adds sleeps, retries, larger production budgets or
weaker equality checks.

Hosted run 40656917043 preserved a live process with no first-UI marker after
the fixed three-second delay; that receipt cannot distinguish slow cold
renderer initialization from a renderer failure. The harness therefore
instruments that hypothesis without changing acceptance: the existing
three-second grace now observes the marker once per second and records its
actual duration plus poll 1, 2 or 3 when first seen. The original exact-process,
screenshot/PNG, second-liveness and final-marker sequence remains, including
acceptance when the marker is first observed post-capture. No command deadline,
retry or pass/fail guard changes before #303's required attribution. A process
that never reaches first UI or dies still produces failed evidence.
Pre-capture liveness failure retains readiness as `not-attempted` and blocks
the screenshot exactly as before.
The in-memory readiness record is created before capture, so controlled
screenshot stalls and invalid PNGs retain timing and capture errors when the
attempt unwinds and writes its manifest. A hard job termination can still
preempt that write.
Independent `simctl install`, launch and screenshot command stalls remain
failed experimental evidence under #303; their deadlines, test-owned-device
guards and non-gating nightly/manual policy are unchanged.

## Qualifying native Markdown scrolling before claiming a WARP repair

The small offscreen Markdown benchmark did not install the application's
software-background plugins. Its measurements remain useful for that harness,
but could not select a native rendering repair. An isolated, owned-window
probe now opens synthetic Markdown through the normal application route,
requires actual scroll-offset movement and the DX12 CPU adapter, and records
bounded host timing and retention counters. Early zero-motion attempts were
rejected rather than counted as navigation evidence.

Native control pilots confirmed substantial rendering CPU during movement.
The ADR 0051 implementation retains an exact static leading paint-job prefix
and repaints the changing suffix, keeping existing image/signature bounds,
admission, callback preparation, immutable queued resources and idle reuse.
Pixel/resource regressions pass, including fractional clips, translucency,
suffix buffer indexing and copy-only targets. Partial reuse is not reported
as an exact no-paint hit.

The Windows desktop initially disconnected before comparison, so publication
remained draft. Follow-up review added plain Edit qualification and repaired
private profiler ACL persistence without requiring an audit privilege or
weakening owner/SYSTEM-only access. Twenty source-matched native observations,
balanced per case, now show 32.5-53.5% lower process CPU across Markdown and
40.8% in Edit. Two earlier prose settle-timeout rejections and adverse table
tail/drift results remain explicit in
[the WARP record](../validation/windows-warp/README.md#native-prefix-navigation-2026-10-28).
The owner accepted the renderer-wide ADR 0051 policy on 2026-10-28. Physical
latency, broader surfaces/hardware/resources and freeze-repair claims remain
unqualified; architectural acceptance is not milestone acceptance.

## Keeping headless Vulkan tests out of the loader's debug-name fault

The required Ubuntu application tests were dying mid-suite with SIGSEGV on
main and on the WARP follow-up, before Rust could report a test outcome.
A local Linux reproduction under GDB identified the Vulkan loader's
`vkSetDebugUtilsObjectNameEXT` path while naming a staging buffer, not
application shutdown. Hosted Linux validation now omits optional native object
naming while explicitly retaining wgpu validation and the real software Vulkan
pixel tests. Native validation remains requested, but this configuration does
not claim that Vulkan validation-layer messages are routed through wgpu while
native debug utilities are disabled. It does not skip, serialize or retry the
suite, change rendering algorithms, or alter production renderer defaults. The
underlying loader fault is not claimed repaired by this test-environment
configuration.

## Keeping document scroll positions independent

Opening another Markdown document also exposed window-local scroll IDs: new
documents inherited the last one's offset. Editor Preview/Edit scroll regions
now include document identity, and standalone viewer regions include tab
identity. A switching regression verifies new-at-top and restoration of the
first document's own position without resetting scroll on every frame.

## Drawing Markdown table column dividers once

The table renderer submitted each full-height internal column border again
for every row, even though warm layout preparation already reused the cell
measurements. It now submits each column boundary once at the same border
overlay stage and inside the same horizontal scroll clip. Row separators,
prepared widths/heights and live selectable cells retain their existing path.

Focused CPU regressions inspect emitted geometry for tall, ragged-source,
empty-cell and header-only tables, normal/fractional scale and horizontal
scrolling. This establishes column-scaled divider submission, not measured
